    * Could make one of the P runs into Z and add P? same.
  So for D even, v=1, u=0: r=C+1, inc=C+1.
- u=1, v=0:
  Leading run at 0 can be P (if C>0) or Z. To minimize r, make leading P. Then need C-1 additional P runs. r=C, inc=C-1. Feasible if C>=1. If C=1, r=1 (just leading P), inc0, v=0 (run not touching end) feasible if D>1. Good.
- u=1, v=1:
  Leading P. Last trailing constraint:
    If D odd: trailing can be P. We can have one run be both leading and trailing if C=1 and D odd? A single P run from 0 to D-1 requires length D odd, contribution +1, yes. Then r=1, inc0. If C>1, need C P runs, last P, r=C, inc=C-1.
    If D even: trailing cannot be P. Need adjust. Leading P contributes +1. Need total C. We can have trailing Z (0) and p=C P runs? Wait leading P is one of p. If trailing Z, then p=C (including leading), z=1, r=C+1, inc=(C+1)-1=C. Alternatively trailing N (-1), need p=C+1, n=1, r=C+2, inc=C+1. So minimal r=C+1, inc=C.
  So for D even, v=1, u=1: inc=C.

Case C = 0:
Need total 0. Minimal r?
- u=0, v=0: r=0 (all zeros), inc=0.
- u=1, v=0: Need leading run. Leading run can be Z (0) or P (+1). To keep C=0, if leading P, need an N run, r=2, inc=1 (since u=1, inc=r-1=1). If leading Z, r=1, inc=0. So choose leading Z. Feasible: leading run even length, not touching end, e.g., y=1,1,0,... if D>2. inc=0.
- u=0, v=1: Need trailing run. Trailing can be Z (0) always? D even trailing Z start even; D odd trailing Z start odd. Yes Z possible. r=1, inc=1 (u=0). Could we do r=0? v=1 requires ones, so no. inc=1.
- u=1, v=1: Need both leading and trailing. Could be a single run from 0 to D-1. Its contribution is:
    D even: length even -> Z, C=0. r=1, inc=0.
    D odd: length odd -> P, C=1, not 0. Need adjust. Options: leading Z and trailing Z? That would be two runs? If leading run Z not touching end, trailing Z, r=2, inc=1 (u=1 => inc=r-1=1). But D can be small (e.g., A+B=1). Need ensure formula not negative and feasible.

Case C < 0:
Need total negative. Let M = -C >0. Prefer N runs (-1). Minimal r = M, inc = r - u.
Boundary:
- u=0: no leading. v=0: no trailing. r=M N runs, inc=M. Feasible if D enough.
- u=0, v=1:
  Trailing run when D even can be N (start odd, odd length). So all M N runs, last N, r=M, inc=M. When D odd, trailing cannot be N (only P or Z). Need adjust. - u=1, v=0:
  Leading run at 0 cannot be N. It can be Z (0) or P (+1). To minimize r, choose leading Z (0), then need M N runs, r=M+1, inc=(M+1)-1=M. If leading P, need M+1 N runs, r=M+2, inc=M+1. So inc=M.
- u=1, v=1:
  Leading Z, trailing:
    If D even: trailing can be N. Then r = 1 (leading Z) + M (N runs) = M+1, inc = (M+1)-1 = M.
    If D odd: trailing cannot be N. Need trailing Z and M N runs, plus leading Z: r = M+2, inc = M+1. Could we use a single run that is both leading and trailing? D odd single run P contribution +1, not negative. So no.
  So for D odd, v=1: inc=M+1.

Now, we need compute start_adj_value and end_adj_value.

Given start tile k_s, end tile k_t.

Start constraint applies if u=1. The required start value val_s:
- If O0=0 (H): first move vertical, sign = v_sign. val_s = high if v_sign=+1 else low.
- If O0=1 (V): first move horizontal, sign = h_sign. val_s = high if h_sign=+1 else low.
So val_s = high if (O0==0 ? v_sign : h_sign) == +1 else low.

Start_adj = dist_k(k_s, val_s), where dist_k(k, boundary) = min(abs(k - boundary), 2) for K>2? For K=2, boundary values 0/1, abs is 0/1, min works. For K>2, boundary 0 or K-1. If k is intermediate, distance to boundary via direct internal is |k-boundary|, via neighbor detour is 2. So min(|k-boundary|, 2). But is via neighbor detour always possible from start block? Yes, to change k within start block, can move to an adjacent block and back, cost 2. This is allowed before first block move. So dist_k = min(abs(k - boundary), 2). For K=2, min(abs,2)=abs. Good.

End constraint applies if v=0. Required end value val_t:
- If OD=0 (H): last move vertical, sign = v_sign. End target H: val_t = low if v_sign=+1 else high.
- If OD=1 (V): last move horizontal, sign = h_sign. End target V: val_t = low if h_sign=+1 else high.
So val_t = low if (OD==0 ? v_sign : h_sign) == +1 else high.

End_adj = dist_k(k_t, val_t).

Now, we need compute O0, OD, A, B, h_sign, v_sign.

Block coordinates:
Given point (X,Y) integer Sx,Sy. i = floor_div(X, K), j = floor_div(Y, K). rem_x = X - i*K, rem_y = Y - j*K.
Orientation O = 0 if (i+j)%2==0 (H) else 1 (V).
Tile index k:
- If O==0 (H): k = rem_y.
- If O==1 (V): k = rem_x.

For end similarly.

A = abs(i2 - i1), B = abs(j2 - j1). h_sign = 1 if i2>i1 else -1 if i2<i1 else 0. v_sign = 1 if j2>j1 else -1 if j2<j1 else 0.
If A=0, h_sign=0; if B=0, v_sign=0.

D = A+B.

If D==0: answer = min(abs(k_s - k_t), 2) (since same block orientation? Wait if same block, orientation same, k indices both along same axis. Yes.)

Moving to adjacent block and back to same block changes k to desired boundary. Let's verify for H block: H_0 -> V_{K-1} (left neighbor) -> H_{K-1} cost2. Is H_0 adjacent to V_{K-1}? H bottom? Then V_{K-1} to H_{K-1}: V_{K-1} right edge at x=iK, H_{K-1} left edge y [jK+K-1,jK+K]; share segment. So cost2. Similarly for V block. Good.

Now, we should also consider if start/end k adjustment via neighbor detour might interfere with the block path? It adds 2 moves but returns to same block. Our extra cost model adds S_adj/E_adj and straight pass costs separately. If we pay S_adj at start, we do internal/detour moves before first block move. This doesn't affect y sequence or block path. Good.

But wait, if start_adj uses direct internal move of cost |k_s - val_s| which could be 1, that's one move staying in same block. Our distance lower bound D + extra counts it. Fine.

Now, we need confirm that the straight pass cost c*inc is indeed the extra cost for same-axis adjacent pairs. Each costly pair corresponds to a straight pass through a block requiring k change from low to high or high to low, cost c. But if the block path goes straight through a constrained block, the required k values at entry and exit are low/high. We assumed cost c. However, could we avoid this cost by choosing the k at the middle block differently? No, entry and exit constraints fix both sides; must change k within that block. Cost c. Could the change be done by using the same neighbor detour that might also serve as a block move? No, detour goes to side neighbor and back, adding 2 moves. So extra c.

But what if the two consecutive same-axis moves are not both in the same direction? In a monotone path, same-axis moves are always same sign (all E or all W, all N or all S). So yes.

Now, need ensure the y optimization's inc counts exactly the number of costly same-axis pairs. We derived cost condition for a pair of symbol s at start index m: cost if s == e_m. In y, this corresponds to y_m=0,y_{m+1}=1. Let's re-derive to be absolutely sure.

Given s_m = y_m xor e_m. e_{m+1}=1-e_m.
If s_m = s_{m+1} (same axis pair):
Then y_m xor e_m = y_{m+1} xor (1-e_m).
Let y_{m+1} = y_m xor e_m xor (1-e_m) = y_m xor 1. So y_{m+1}=1-y_m.
Cost condition for pair of symbol s at m: s_m == e_m (since e_m = O0 xor m%2, and cost if m%2 = O0 xor s => s = O0 xor m%2 = e_m). If s_m = e_m, then y_m = s_m xor e_m =0. Thus y_m=0,y_{m+1}=1. Yes. If s_m != e_m, y_m=1,y_{m+1}=0, no cost. If s_m != s_{m+1} (turn), then y_m = y_{m+1} (as shown), no pair. So inc = #0->1 transitions in y. Perfect.

Now, endpoint constraints:
Start adj applies if first move axis is constrained. First move s0. Start constrained axis = 1-O0. Condition s0 = 1-O0. s0 = y0 xor e0 = y0 xor O0. So y0 xor O0 = 1-O0 => y0=1. Yes.
End adj applies if last move axis s_last = 1-OD. e_last = O0 xor ((D-1)%2). We derived y_last=0. Let's verify with sample2 u=1,v=1: y_last=1, no end adj. Last move s_last = y_last xor e_last = 1 xor e_last. For O0=0,D=4,e_last=0 xor1=1, s_last=0 horizontal. End OD=0 H, constrained axis vertical, so no end adj. Good.
Sample1 u=1,v=0: y_last=0, end adj applies. Last move s_last = 0 xor e_last. O0=1,D=3,e_last=1 xor0? D-1=2 even, e=O0=1, s_last=1 vertical. End OD=0 H, constrained vertical, yes end adj. Good.

Now, we need compute OD. OD = O0 xor (D & 1) because each move flips orientation. Since D = A+B. Use OD = O0 ^ (D % 2).

Now, let's double-check the derivation of C and inc formulas with the y transformation for all O0.

We defined e_m = O0 xor (m%2). s_m = y_m xor e_m. Counts:
A = #s=0.
We derived:
If O0=0: A = E + b - a => C = a-b = E - A.
If O0=1: A = Od + a - b => C = a-b = A - Od.
Let's re-derive carefully to avoid sign error.

O0=0:
e_m = m%2 (0 even,1 odd).
s = y xor e.
Even positions e=0: s=y. So s=0 count on even = E - a.
Odd positions e=1: s=1-y. s=0 iff y=1. Count = b.
Total A = (E - a) + b.
Thus a - b = E - A. Yes.

O0=1:
e_m = 1 xor (m%2): even e=1, odd e=0.
Even: s=1-y, s=0 iff y=1. Count = a.
Odd: s=y, s=0 iff y=0. Count = Od - b.
Total A = a + (Od - b).
Thus a - b = A - Od. Yes.

Good.

Now, we need implement inc_min formula robustly. Let's write a function min_inc(C, D, u, v):
if C > 0:
    if u == 0:
        if v == 0:
            return C
        else: # v=1
            return C if D % 2 == 1 else C + 1
    else: # u=1
        if v == 0:
            return C - 1
        else:
            return C - 1 if D % 2 == 1 else C
elif C == 0:
    if u == 0:
        if v == 0:
            return 0
        else:
            return 1
    else:
        if v == 0:
            return 0
        else:
            return 0 if D % 2 == 0 else 1
else: # C < 0
    M = -C
    if u == 0:
        if v == 0:
            return M
        else:
            return M if D % 2 == 0 else M + 1
    else:
        if v == 0:
            return M
        else:
            return M if D % 2 == 0 else M + 1

Let's verify C=0,u=1,v=1,D odd formula returns1. We saw D=3 C=0 u=1,v=1 infeasible, but if feasible for larger D? Example D=5,C=0,u=1,v=1. Can we achieve inc1? y=1,1,0,0,1? positions: a even 0,4 =2; b odd 1=1 => C=1 not0. Need C=0. Try y=1,1,0,1,1: a=2 (0,4), b=2 (1,3), C=0, inc? 1->1,1->0 (1->0),0->1 (0->1 inc),1->1 => inc1. Yes. So formula1. Good.

Now, feasibility function:
def feasible(u,v):
    # counts a,b
    bmin = max(0, -C)
    bmax = min(Od, E - C)
    if bmin > bmax: return False
    lower = bmin
    upper = bmax
    if u == 1:
        lower = max(lower, 1 - C)  # a >=1 => b >= 1-C
    else:
        upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)  # a <= E-1 => b <= E-1-C
    if v == 1:
        if D % 2 == 1: # last even
            lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
        else: # last odd
            lower = max(lower, 1)
    else:
        if D % 2 == 1: # last even zero
            upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
        else: # last odd zero
            upper = min(upper, Od - 1)
    return lower <= upper

Need consider E=0? D>=1 so E>=1. Od can be0. If Od=0 and v=1 with D even? D even and Od=0 only D=0, not possible. D=1 Od=0, D odd. So Od-1 could be -1 if Od=0 and v=0 D even? no.

Let's test D=1, C? Suppose O0=0,A=1,B=0,D=1,E=1,Od=0,C=E-A=0. u=v=0 feasible? bmin=0,bmax=min(0,1)=0. u=0 upper=min(0, E-1-C=0) ok. v=0 D odd upper=min(0,E-1-C=0) ok. feasible. u=v=1? u=1 lower=max(0,1-C=1)=1, bmax=0 -> infeasible. Good, because y0=1 would give A? For A=1,B=0, s=0, e=0, y=0, so u=0.

Another test D=2, C=1, u=1,v=0. E=1,Od=1. bmin=0,bmax=min(1,0)=0. u=1 lower=max(0,0)=0. v=0 D even upper=min(0, Od-1=0)=0. feasible. u=1,v=1: v=1 D even lower=max(0,1)=1 > bmax0 infeasible. Good.

Pseudo:

import sys

def tile_info(X,Y,K):
    i = X // K
    j = Y // K
    rx = X - i*K
    ry = Y - j*K
    if (i + j) % 2 == 0:
        o = 0
        k = ry
    else:
        o = 1
        k = rx
    return i,j,o,k

def dist_k(k, val, K):
    # val is 0 or K-1
    d = abs(k - val)
    if d > 2:
        return 2
    return d
Actually for K=2, d max1. For K>2, d can be >2. min(d,2). So return min(abs(k-val), 2).

def solve_case(K, Sx,Sy,Tx,Ty):
    i1,j1,o1,k1 = tile_info(Sx,Sy,K)
    i2,j2,o2,k2 = tile_info(Tx,Ty,K)
    A = abs(i2-i1)
    B = abs(j2-j1)
    D = A+B
    if D == 0:
        return min(abs(k1-k2), 2)
    h_sign = 0
    if i2 > i1: h_sign = 1
    elif i2 < i1: h_sign = -1
    v_sign = 0
    if j2 > j1: v_sign = 1
    elif j2 < j1: v_sign = -1

    E = (D + 1)//2
    Od = D//2
    if o1 == 0:
        C = E - A
    else:
        C = A - Od

    # endpoint vals
    if o1 == 0:
        sign_first = v_sign
    else:
        sign_first = h_sign
    if sign_first == 1:
        val_s = K - 1
    else:
        val_s = 0
    S_adj = dist_k(k1, val_s, K)  # only if u=1

    oD = o1 ^ (D & 1)
    if oD == 0:
        sign_last = v_sign
    else:
        sign_last = h_sign
    if sign_last == 1:
        val_t = 0  # end target positive -> low
    else:
        val_t = K - 1  # negative -> high
    E_adj = dist_k(k2, val_t, K)  # only if v=0

    c = 1 if K == 2 else 2

    def feasible(u,v):
        bmin = max(0, -C)
        bmax = min(Od, E - C)
        if bmin > bmax:
            return False
        lower = bmin
        upper = bmax
        if u == 1:
            lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
        else:
            upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
        if v == 1:
            if D % 2 == 1:
                lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
            else:
                lower = max(lower, 1)
        else:
            if D % 2 == 1:
                upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
            else:
                upper = min(upper, Od - 1)
        return lower <= upper

    def min_inc(u,v):
        if C > 0:
            if u == 0:
                if v == 0:
                    return C
                else:
                    return C if (D & 1) else C + 1
            else:
                if v == 0:
                    return C - 1
                else:
                    return C - 1 if (D & 1) else C
        elif C == 0:
            if u == 0:
                return 0 if v == 0 else 1
            else:
                if v == 0:
                    return 0
                else:
                    return 0 if (D % 2 == 0) else 1
        else:
            M = -C
            if u == 0:
                if v == 0:
                    return M
                else:
                    return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1
            else:
                if v == 0:
                    return M
                else:
                    return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1

    best = None
    for u in (0,1):
        for v in (0,1):
            if not feasible(u,v):
                continue
            inc = min_inc(u,v)
            extra = c * inc
            if u == 1:
                extra += S_adj
            if v == 0:
                extra += E_adj
            if best is None or extra < best:
                best = extra
    return D + best

Now, let's consider if the tile graph distance could be less than D + extra due to using non-monotone block paths? Any path from block A to B must have at least D block moves, but could include extra block moves in opposite directions and still maybe reduce k adjustment costs? Our model already allows extra moves via endpoint adjustments and straight pass costs (detours) that may include backtracking? Wait our extra costs are added to D but are they always realizable without changing the monotone block path? For straight pass cost c, we said change k within the middle block via a side neighbor and back, adding 2 moves but returning to same block. This is a detour to a side neighbor, which is a block move in a direction perpendicular to the path, then back. This adds 2 moves but does not change the block sequence. So total block moves = D + extra (where extra includes these detours and internal moves). Could a non-monotone path with fewer total moves exist by using a different route that avoids straight passes and endpoint adjustments with less than our computed extra? Our optimization over all monotone paths plus detours should find the shortest. But what if a non-monotone path has block move count >D but fewer k adjustments, total less than D+extra? That is exactly captured by extra: any extra block moves beyond D are either detours to adjust k or backtracking. Could backtracking (moving away then back) reduce the number of costly straight passes more cheaply than our detour cost? Our straight pass cost c is already the cost to adjust k in the middle block via a detour to a side neighbor and back. That's 2 moves for K>2. If instead we backtrack along the path, maybe cost also 2. So c is minimal. Endpoint adjustments use dist_k min(direct,2). Could a non-monotonic first move reduce start adjustment? For example, instead of adjusting k in start block, move to a neighbor and then to another path, maybe cost less? But moving to a neighbor changes block coordinates, adding at least 1 block move; our dist_k via neighbor detour cost 2 returns to start. If you don't return, you're on a different block path; that path is just a different monotone path? If you move opposite to target first, that's a non-monotone path and adds at least 2 block moves (backtrack) compared to D. Could it reduce endpoint adjustment from 2 to 1? Suppose start k far from required boundary, dist_k=2. Instead of paying 2 to adjust, you could take a first move in the unconstrained direction (if available) and later adjust? That is captured by choosing a different u (first move axis) with no start adj. If no unconstrained first move available (e.g., only one axis), you must pay. Could move opposite direction first to a neighbor, then eventually go around, maybe total D+1 instead of D+2? Let's test a case: A=1,B=0, start H_0, end V_0 right. Start adj? First move horizontal unconstrained for H, so no adj. Not issue. Start H_0, end V above (only vertical). Start adj 2, answer3. Could there be path length2 by moving left/right? Start H_0 at (0,0), end V_? at (0,1). Need reach block above. H_0 to H_2 cost2 then cross cost1 =3. Could go H_0 -> V left (0? block (-1,0)) cost1, then maybe to H above? No, block (-1,0) V, to H (0,1)? Not adjacent diagonal. Need more. So 3 minimal. Good.

What about straight pass cost c=2. Could avoid by taking a turn (different order) with no straight pass, which our optimization considers. If unavoidable (e.g., A=2,B=0), straight pass cost c. Could a non-monotone path with block moves D+1 avoid the straight pass and be shorter? Example start H_0 to H_0 with A=2,B=0, we found distance4 = D2 + c2. Could there be path length3? Now, let's consider if inc_min formula could produce inc that is not achievable with the given counts due to run count parity constraints beyond feasibility. We tested small D. The run contribution analysis should be sufficient. Let's prove to ourselves: Given u,v,C,D, the minimal inc is as formulas. We derived from run types and boundary. Feasibility ensures counts can be met. The formulas choose run counts r = inc + u. The number of runs of each type needed:
- If C>0:
  u=0,v=0: r=C all P runs. inc=C.
  u=0,v=1,D odd: r=C all P, last P. inc=C.
  u=0,v=1,D even: r=C+1: C P runs + 1 Z trailing. inc=C+1.
  u=1,v=0: r=C: leading P + C-1 P. inc=C-1.
  u=1,v=1,D odd: r=C: leading P + ... last P. inc=C-1.
  u=1,v=1,D even: r=C+1: leading P + C-1? Wait inc=C, u=1 => r=C+1. Need total C. With leading P, need C-1 more P and one Z trailing: p=C, z=1, r=C+1. Yes.
- C=0:
  u=0,v=0: r=0.
  u=0,v=1: r=1 Z trailing, inc1.
  u=1,v=0: r=1 Z leading, inc0.
  u=1,v=1,D even: r=1 Z run from 0 to D-1 (even length), inc0.
  u=1,v=1,D odd: r=2: leading Z + trailing Z, inc1. (Since single run would be P, not Z.)
- C<0:
  M=-C.
  u=0,v=0: r=M N runs, inc=M.

Code:

import sys

def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.read().strip().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        K = int(next(it)); Sx = int(next(it)); Sy = int(next(it)); Tx = int(next(it)); Ty = int(next(it))
        # tile info
        i1 = Sx // K
        j1 = Sy // K
        rx1 = Sx - i1*K
        ry1 = Sy - j1*K
        if (i1 + j1) & 1:
            o1 = 1
            k1 = rx1
        else:
            o1 = 0
            k1 = ry1

        i2 = Tx // K
        j2 = Ty // K
        rx2 = Tx - i2*K
        ry2 = Ty - j2*K
        if (i2 + j2) & 1:
            o2 = 1
            k2 = rx2
        else:
            o2 = 0
            k2 = ry2

        A = abs(i2 - i1)
        B = abs(j2 - j1)
        D = A + B
        if D == 0:
            out.append(str(min(abs(k1 - k2), 2)))
            continue

        h_sign = 1 if i2 > i1 else (-1 if i2 < i1 else 0)
        v_sign = 1 if j2 > j1 else (-1 if j2 < j1 else 0)

        E = (D + 1) // 2
        Od = D // 2
        if o1 == 0:
            C = E - A
        else:
            C = A - Od

        # start adj
        sign_first = v_sign if o1 == 0 else h_sign
        val_s = K - 1 if sign_first == 1 else 0
        S_adj = min(abs(k1 - val_s), 2)

        oD = o1 ^ (D & 1)
        sign_last = v_sign if oD == 0 else h_sign
        val_t = 0 if sign_last == 1 else K - 1
        E_adj = min(abs(k2 - val_t), 2)

        c = 1 if K == 2 else 2

        def feasible(u, v):
            bmin = max(0, -C)
            bmax = min(Od, E - C)
            if bmin > bmax:
                return False
            lower = bmin
            upper = bmax
            if u == 1:
                lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
            else:
                upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
            if v == 1:
                if D & 1:
                    lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
                else:
                    lower = max(lower, 1)
            else:
                if D & 1:
                    upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
                else:
                    upper = min(upper, Od - 1)
            # double endpoints for odd D
            if (D & 1) and D >= 3:
                if u == 1 and v == 1:
                    lower = max(lower, 2 - C)
                if u == 0 and v == 0:
                    upper = min(upper, E - 2 - C)
            return lower <= upper

        def min_inc(u, v):
            if C > 0:
                if u == 0:
                    if v == 0:
                        return C
                    else:
                        return C if (D & 1) else C + 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return C - 1
                    else:
                        return C - 1 if (D & 1) else C
            elif C == 0:
                if u == 0:
                    return 0 if v == 0 else 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return 0
                    else:
                        return 0 if (D % 2 == 0) else 1
            else:
                M = -C
                if u == 0:
                    if v == 0:
                        return M
                    else:
                        return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return M
                    else:
                        return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1

        best = None
        for u in (0,1):
            for v in (0,1):
                if not feasible(u,v):
                    continue
                inc = min_inc(u,v)
                extra = c * inc
                if u == 1:
                    extra += S_adj
                if v == 0:
                    extra += E_adj
                if best is None or extra < best:
                    best = extra

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.read().strip().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    ans = []

    for _ in range(T):
        K = int(next(it))
        Sx = int(next(it))
        Sy = int(next(it))
        Tx = int(next(it))
        Ty = int(next(it))

        i1 = Sx // K
        j1 = Sy // K
        rx1 = Sx - i1 * K
        ry1 = Sy - j1 * K
        if (i1 + j1) & 1:
            o1 = 1
            k1 = rx1
        else:
            o1 = 0
            k1 = ry1

        i2 = Tx // K
        j2 = Ty // K
        rx2 = Tx - i2 * K
        ry2 = Ty - j2 * K
        if (i2 + j2) & 1:
            k2 = rx2
        else:
            k2 = ry2

        A = abs(i2 - i1)
        B = abs(j2 - j1)
        D = A + B

        if D == 0:
            ans.append(str(min(abs(k1 - k2), 2)))
            continue

        h_sign = 1 if i2 > i1 else (-1 if i2 < i1 else 0)
        v_sign = 1 if j2 > j1 else (-1 if j2 < j1 else 0)

        E = (D + 1) // 2
        Od = D // 2

        if o1 == 0:
            C = E - A
        else:
            C = A - Od

        sign_first = v_sign if o1 == 0 else h_sign
        val_s = K - 1 if sign_first == 1 else 0
        S_adj = min(abs(k1 - val_s), 2)

        oD = o1 ^ (D & 1)
        sign_last = v_sign if oD == 0 else h_sign
        val_t = 0 if sign_last == 1 else K - 1
        E_adj = min(abs(k2 - val_t), 2)

        c = min(K - 1, 2)

        def feasible(u, v):
            bmin = max(0, -C)
            bmax = min(Od, E - C)
            if bmin > bmax:
                return False

            lower = bmin
            upper = bmax

            if u == 1:
                lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
            else:
                upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)

            if v == 1:
                if D & 1:
                    lower = max(lower, 1 - C)
                else:
                    lower = max(lower, 1)
            else:
                if D & 1:
                    upper = min(upper, E - 1 - C)
                else:
                    upper = min(upper, Od - 1)

            if (D & 1) and D >= 3:
                if u == 1 and v == 1:
                    lower = max(lower, 2 - C)
                if u == 0 and v == 0:
                    upper = min(upper, E - 2 - C)

            return lower <= upper

        def min_inc(u, v):
            if C > 0:
                if u == 0:
                    if v == 0:
                        return C
                    return C if (D & 1) else C + 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return C - 1
                    return C - 1 if (D & 1) else C
            elif C == 0:
                if u == 0:
                    return 0 if v == 0 else 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return 0
                    return 0 if (D % 2 == 0) else 1
            else:
                M = -C
                if u == 0:
                    if v == 0:
                        return M
                    return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1
                else:
                    if v == 0:
                        return M
                    return M if (D % 2 == 0) else M + 1

        best = None
        for u in (0, 1):
            for v in (0, 1):
                if not feasible(u, v):
                    continue
                inc = min_inc(u, v)
                extra = c * inc
                if u == 1:
                    extra += S_adj
                if v == 0:
                    extra += E_adj
                if best is None or extra < best:
                    best = extra

        ans.append(str(D + best))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
